= color_style()\n        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)\n\n    def format(self, record):\n        msg = record.msg\n        status_code = getattr(record, \"status_code\", None)\n\n        if status_code:\n            if 200 <= status_code < 300:\n                # Put 2XX first, since it should be the common case\n                msg = self.style.HTTP_SUCCESS(msg)\n            elif 100 <= status_code < 200:\n                msg = self.style.HTTP_INFO(msg)\n            elif status_code == 304:\n                msg = self.style.HTTP_NOT_MODIFIED(msg)\n            elif 300 <= status_code < 400:\n                msg = self.style.HTTP_REDIRECT(msg)\n            elif status_code == 404:\n                msg = self.style.HTTP_NOT_FOUND(msg)\n            elif 400 <= status_code < 500:\n                msg = self.style.HTTP_BAD_REQUEST(msg)\n            else:\n                # Any 5XX, or any other status code\n                msg = self.style.HTTP_SERVER_ERROR(msg)\n\n        if self.uses_server_time() and not hasattr(record, \"server_time\"):\n            record.server_time = self.formatTime(record, self.datefmt)\n\n        record.msg = msg\n        return super().format(record)\n\n    def uses_server_time(self):\n        return self._fmt.find(\"{server_time}\") >= 0\n\n\ndef log_response(\n    message,\n    *args,\n    response=None,\n    request=None,\n    logger=request_logger,\n    level=None,\n    exception=None,\n):\n    \"\"\"\n    Log errors based on HttpResponse status.\n\n    Log 5xx responses as errors and 4xx responses as warnings (unless a level\n    is given as a keyword argument). The HttpResponse status_code and the\n    request are passed to the logger's extra parameter.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Check if the response has already been logged. Multiple requests to log\n    # the same response can be received in some cases, e.g., when the\n    # response is the result of an exception and is logged when the exception\n    # is caught, to record the exception.\n    if getattr(response, \"_has_been_logged\", False):\n        return\n\n    if level is None:\n        if response.status_code >= 500:\n            level = \"error\"\n        elif response.status_code >= 400:\n            level = \"warning\"\n        else:\n            level = \"info\"\n\n    getattr(logger, level)(\n        message,\n        *args,\n        extra={\n            \"status_code\": response.status_code,\n            \"request\": request,\n        },\n        exc_info=exception,\n    )\n    response._has_been_logged = True\n","numLines":258,"startLine":1,"totalLines":258}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01GRKiM6sWeq2p5Cmw1uME8U","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01FJsWo2qxgeAvMwUZuC3brq","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/investigation.md","content":"# Investigation Report: ADMINS/MANAGERS Settings Format Migration Audit\n\n## Summary\n\nThe migration from `[(name, email), ...]` tuples to `[email, ...]` strings for the `ADMINS` and `MANAGERS` settings (ticket #36138) has already been implemented on Django's default branch (commits `e295033`, `90fc762`, `a8536e3`), but this local codebase (Django 6.0 alpha, branch HEAD) still uses the **old tuple-only format** in `_send_server_message()`. The migration touches the core mail module, logging handler, 6 test files, and 3 documentation files. The latest branch adds a `RemovedInDjango70Warning` deprecation for tuple usage and changes the error type from `ValueError` to `ImproperlyConfigured`.\n\n## Root Cause\n\nThe core code path that consumes the tuple format is the `_send_server_message()` function in `django/core/mail/__init__.py`. Both `mail_admins()` and `mail_managers()` delegate to this single function. It reads the setting value, validates the format, and extracts email addresses.\n\n### Local (old) code path (`django/core/mail/__init__.py:122-147`):\n\n```python\ndef _send_server_message(*, setting_name, subject, message, html_message=None,\n                         fail_silently=False, connection=None):\n    recipients = getattr(settings, setting_name)\n    if not recipients:\n        return\n    # Requires 2-tuples only:\n    if not all(isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2 for a in recipients):\n        raise ValueError(f\"The {setting_name} setting must be a list of 2-tuples.\")\n    mail = EmailMultiAlternatives(\n        subject=\"%s%s\" % (settings.EMAIL_SUBJECT_PREFIX, subject),\n        body=message,\n        from_email=settings.SERVER_EMAIL,\n        to=[a[1] for a in recipients],  # <-- extracts email from tuple[1]\n        connection=connection,\n    )\n```\n\n### Migrated (new) code path on default branch:\n\n```python\ndef _send_server_message(...):\n    recipients = getattr(settings, setting_name)\n    if not recipients:\n        return\n    # Deprecation shim for old tuple format:\n    if all(isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2 for a in recipients):\n        warnings.warn(\n            f\"Using (name, address) pairs in the {setting_name} setting is deprecated.\"\n            \" Replace with a list of email address strings.\",\n            RemovedInDjango70Warning, stacklevel=2,\n        )\n        recipients = [a[1] for a in recipients]\n    # New validation for string list format:\n    if not isinstance(recipients, (list, tuple)) or not all(\n        isinstance(address, (str, Promise)) for address in recipients\n    ):\n        raise ImproperlyConfigured(\n            f\"The {setting_name} setting must be a list of email address strings.\"\n        )\n    mail = EmailMultiAlternatives(\n        ..., to=recipients, ...  # <-- uses string list directly\n    )\n```\n\n## Evidence\n\n### 1. Files That Read `settings.ADMINS` or `settings.MANAGERS`\n\n| File | Line(s) | How It Reads the Setting |\n|------|---------|--------------------------|\n| `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 131, 135, 142 | `getattr(settings, setting_name)` in `_send_server_message()`. Validates as 2-tuples, extracts `a[1]` for email. |\n| `django/utils/log.py` | 97 | `AdminEmailHandler.emit()` checks `not settings.ADMINS` for early return (truthiness only, no format assumption). |\n| `django/middleware/common.py` | 129-142 | `BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware.process_response()` calls `mail_managers()` -- indirect consumer. |\n| `django/core/management/commands/sendtestemail.py` | 43-46 | Calls `mail_managers()` and `mail_admins()` -- indirect consumer. |\n\n### 2. Files That Validate or Type-Check the Settings Format\n\n| File | Line(s) | Validation |\n|------|---------|------------|\n| `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 135-136 (local) | `if not all(isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2 for a in recipients)` raises `ValueError`. On default branch: changed to deprecation warning + `ImproperlyConfigured` for non-string-list. |\n\n**Note:** There is NO system check (`django/core/checks/`) that validates the format of `ADMINS`/`MANAGERS`. Validation only happens at mail-send time.\n\n### 3. Files That Unpack the Tuple Format\n\n| File | Line(s) | Pattern |\n|------|---------|---------|\n| `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 142 (local) | `to=[a[1] for a in recipients]` -- index-based extraction of email from tuple. On default branch: `recipients = [a[1] for a in recipients]` only in the deprecation shim. |\n\nNo other Django source file unpacks the `(name, email)` tuple directly. The name field is **unused** in all Django code paths -- it was historically carried over but never rendered in email headers (the `From:` header uses `SERVER_EMAIL`, not the admin's name).\n\n### 4. Documentation References Showing the Old Tuple Format\n\n| File | Line(s) | Content | Status on Default Branch |\n|------|---------|---------|--------------------------|\n| `docs/ref/settings.txt` | 55-57 (local) | `Each item in the list should be a tuple of (Full name, email address). Example:: [(\"John\", \"john@example.com\"), (\"Mary\", \"mary@example.com\")]` | **Updated** to: `Each item in the list should be an email address string. Example:: ADMINS = [\"john@example.com\", '\"Ng, Mary\" <mary@example.com>']` with `.. versionchanged:: 6.0` note |\n| `docs/ref/settings.txt` | 2066-2075 | `MANAGERS` setting: \"A list in the same format as ADMINS\" | Unchanged (inherits new format from ADMINS reference) |\n| `django/conf/global_settings.py` | 24-26 (local) | Comment: `# People who get code error notifications. In the format # [('Full Name', 'email@example.com'), ('Full Name', 'anotheremail@example.com')]` | **Updated** on default branch to: `# [\"email@example.com\", '\"Full Name\" <anotheremail@example.com>']` (commit `a8536e3`) |\n| `docs/topics/email.txt` | 166-192 | Documents `mail_admins()` and `mail_managers()` with references to ADMINS/MANAGERS settings | No tuple examples here; relies on cross-reference to settings docs |\n| `docs/ref/logging.txt` | 318-319 | `AdminEmailHandler` sends to site ADMINS | Indirect reference, no format shown |\n\n### 5. Third-Party Compatibility Concerns\n\n1. **Users with existing `(name, email)` tuple settings**: On the default branch, these will trigger a `RemovedInDjango70Warning` deprecation warning but will continue to work in Django 6.x. The tuple format will be removed entirely in Django 7.0.\n\n2. **Third-party packages that call `mail_admins()`/`mail_managers()`**: These are unaffected -- the API signatures are the same. The change is internal to how settings are consumed.\n\n3. **Third-party packages that read `settings.ADMINS`/`settings.MANAGERS` directly**: Any code that unpacks `(name, email)` tuples (e.g., `for name, email in settings.ADMINS`) will break when users switch to the new string-list format. This is the primary compatibility concern.\n\n4. **Third-party packages that construct ADMINS/MANAGERS dynamically**: Any code that builds settings values as `[(name, email)]` tuples will get deprecation warnings and eventually break.\n\n5. **The `name` field is lost**: Code that relied on admin names (e.g., for display purposes, custom email templates with admin names) will need to adopt RFC 5322 display-name format: `'\"Full Name\" <email@example.com>'` instead of `(\"Full Name\", \"email@example.com\")`.\n\n6. **Error type change**: The validation error changed from `ValueError` to `ImproperlyConfigured`. Code catching `ValueError` from `mail_admins()`/`mail_managers()` for invalid settings will need updating.\n\n## Affected Components\n\n1. **`django/core/mail/__init__.py`** -- Core: `_send_server_message()`, `mail_admins()`, `mail_managers()`\n2. **`django/utils/log.py`** -- `AdminEmailHandler.emit()` (truthiness check only, no format change needed)\n3. **`django/middleware/common.py`** -- `BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware` (indirect via `mail_managers()`, no change needed)\n4. **`django/core/management/commands/sendtestemail.py`** -- `sendtestemail` command (indirect, no change needed)\n5. **`django/conf/global_settings.py`** -- Default settings comment and value definition\n6. **`docs/ref/settings.txt`** -- ADMINS and MANAGERS settings documentation\n7. **`tests/mail/tests.py`** -- Mail test suite (15+ test methods affected)\n8. **`tests/mail/test_sendtestemail.py`** -- sendtestemail command tests\n9. **`tests/logging_tests/tests.py`** -- AdminEmailHandler tests (8+ override_settings)\n10. **`tests/middleware/tests.py`** -- BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware tests\n11. **`tests/view_tests/tests/test_debug.py`** -- Debug view email tests (3 methods)\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Migration Checklist\n\nThe migration has been implemented in 4 commits on the default branch. The migration touches the core mail module, logging handler, 6 test files, and 3 documentation files. The latest branch adds a `RemovedInDjango70Warning` deprecation for tuple usage and changes the error type from `ValueError` to `ImproperlyConfigured`.\n\n## Root Cause\n\nThe core code path that consumes the tuple format is the `_send_server_message()` function in `django/core/mail/__init__.py`. Both `mail_admins()` and `mail_managers()` delegate to this single function. It reads the setting value, validates the format, and extracts email addresses.\n\n### Local (old) code path (`django/core/mail/__init__.py:122-147`):\n\n```python\ndef _send_server_message(*, setting_name, subject, message, html_message=None,\n                         fail_silently=False, connection=None):\n    recipients = getattr(settings, setting_name)\n    if not recipients:\n        return\n    # Requires 2-tuples only:\n    if not all(isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2 for a in recipients):\n        raise ValueError(f\"The {setting_name} setting must be a list of 2-tuples.\")\n    mail = EmailMultiAlternatives(\n        subject=\"%s%s\" % (settings.EMAIL_SUBJECT_PREFIX, subject),\n        body=message,\n        from_email=settings.SERVER_EMAIL,\n        to=[a[1] for a in recipients],  # <-- extracts email from tuple[1]\n        connection=connection,\n    )\n```\n\n### Migrated (new) code path on default branch:\n\n```python\ndef _send_server_message(...):\n    recipients = getattr(settings, setting_name)\n    if not recipients:\n        return\n    # Deprecation shim for old tuple format:\n    if all(isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2 for a in recipients):\n        warnings.warn(\n            f\"Using (name, address) pairs in the {setting_name} setting is deprecated.\"\n            \" Replace with a list of email address strings.\",\n            RemovedInDjango70Warning, stacklevel=2,\n        )\n        recipients = [a[1] for a in recipients]\n    # New validation for string list format:\n    if not isinstance(recipients, (list, tuple)) or not all(\n        isinstance(address, (str, Promise)) for address in recipients\n    ):\n        raise ImproperlyConfigured(\n            f\"The {setting_name} setting must be a list of email address strings.\"\n        )\n    mail = EmailMultiAlternatives(\n        ..., to=recipients, ...  # <-- uses string list directly\n    )\n```\n\n## Evidence\n\n### 1. Validates as 2-tuples, extracts `a[1]` for email. |\n| `django/utils/log.py` | 97 | `AdminEmailHandler.emit()` checks `not settings.ADMINS` for early return (truthiness only, no format assumption). |\n| `django/middleware/common.py` | 129-142 | `BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware.process_response()` calls `mail_managers()` -- indirect consumer. |\n| `django/core/management/commands/sendtestemail.py` | 43-46 | Calls `mail_managers()` and `mail_admins()` -- indirect consumer. |\n\n### 2. On default branch: changed to deprecation warning + `ImproperlyConfigured` for non-string-list. |\n\n**Note:** There is NO system check (`django/core/checks/`) that validates the format of `ADMINS`/`MANAGERS`. Validation only happens at mail-send time.\n\n### 3. On default branch: `recipients = [a[1] for a in recipients]` only in the deprecation shim. |\n\nNo other Django source file unpacks the `(name, email)` tuple directly. The name field is **unused** in all Django code paths -- it was historically carried over but never rendered in email headers (the `From:` header uses `SERVER_EMAIL`, not the admin's name).\n\n### 4.
